Хотите, чтобы я подробнее расписал логику именно для PoC-версии?\",[[4,26,[[null,null,null,null,2]]],[81,106,[[null,null,null,null,2]]]]]]]]],[2,0,2],\"56fdd199312815e2\",null,null,\"56fdd199312815e2\",null,null,null,\"3 Flash\",null,null,1],[1774489451,485983000],null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,\"28a429f0-5b7a-4302-b895-65920dfcbd6a\"],[[\"c_32920e9e8337e92a\",\"r_187affc451bff39c\"],[\"c_32920e9e8337e92a\",\"r_a3e099662cb86637\",\"rc_a1dc9327f815b1f5\"],[[\"О'кей Но какая тогда практическая, Короче говоря все эти данные. Они же их утере. Это это большая проблема с точки зрения пользовательского опыта. Какая тогда модель схема восстановления Таких данных?\"],2,null,1,\"56fdd199312815e2\",0],[[[\"rc_f85611d81076edd0\",[\"Это отличный вопрос, который вскрывает главную дилемму безопасности: **«Чем сложнее украсть данные, тем легче их потерять самому»**.\\n\\nЕсли мы используем `setInvalidatedByBiometricEnrollment(true)`, мы превращаем смартфон в «черный ящик». Если ключ аннулирован (из-за нового отпечатка или потери телефона), данные, зашифрованные этим ключом, превращаются в цифровой мусор.\\n\\nДля системы **CLAVIS** существует три основных архитектурных модели восстановления (Recovery Flow), которые сохраняют баланс между безопасностью и удобством:\\n\\n---\\n\\n### 1. Модель «Облачного Осколка» (Social/Cloud Recovery)\\nЭто наиболее современный подход, используемый в криптокошельках и системах защиты данных.\\n* **Механизм:** При первой настройке CLAVIS генерирует **Ключ Восстановления** (Recovery Key). Этот ключ разбивается на части (например, по схеме Шамира). \\n    * Одна часть хранится в вашем iCloud/Google Drive (зашифрованная вашим сложным паролем).\\n    * Вторая часть хранится на сервере CLAVIS (привязанная к вашему подтвержденному email/телефону).\\n    * Третья часть — это ваш бумажный QR-код или кодовая фраза.\\n* **Восстановление:** Чтобы восстановить доступ на новом устройстве или после сброса биометрии, вам нужно собрать 2 из 3 частей. Это позволяет восстановить мастер-ключ без участия биометрии старого устройства.\\n\\n### 2. Модель «Доверенного Эмитента» (Issuer Re-issuance)\\nПоскольку CLAVIS работает с **Verifiable Credentials (VC)**, нам не обязательно восстанавливать старые зашифрованные файлы. Мы можем восстановить *права* на них.\\n* **Механизм:** Ваша личность (DID) подтверждена (например, через государственные базы или банк).\\n* **Восстановление:** Если вы потеряли ключ, вы инициируете «Сброс кошелька». Ваше приложение отправляет запросы всем **Эмитентам** (Issuers) — вашему врачу, банку, транспортному департаменту: *«Я подтвердил личность заново, пожалуйста, перевыпустите мои документы на мой новый публичный ключ»*.\\n* **Результат:** Процесс автоматизирован. Через 5 минут у вас снова есть все документы, но подписанные уже для вашего нового аппаратного ключа.\\n\\n### 3. Модель «Холодного Хранилища» (Hardware Backup)\\n* **Механизм:** Пользователь может иметь второе «доверенное устройство» (старый планшет или специальный USB-ключ типа YubiKey).\\n* **Восстановление:** При первичной настройке ключи синхронизируются между двумя устройствами через локальный зашифрованный канал (Bluetooth/NFC). Если основной телефон заблокировал ключи, вы используете второе устройство, чтобы «авторизовать» перевыпуск ключей на телефоне.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Сравнение стратегий для проекта CLAVIS\\n\\n| Метод | Плюсы | Минусы |\\n| :--- | :--- | :--- |\\n| **Ключ-фраза (Seed Phrase)** | Полная автономность. | Пользователи теряют бумажки с фразами. |\\n| **Перевыпуск от Эмитентов** | Самый естественный путь для гос. услуг. | Требует, чтобы все Эмитенты поддерживали протокол CLAVIS. |\\n| **Cloud Split (2 из 3)** | Лучший UX (похоже на «забыл пароль»). | Требует доверия к облачному провайдеру. |\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Резюме для Draft Notes (Implementation Idea)\\n\\n\\u003e **Draft Note: Recovery Protocol**\\n\\u003e Для PoC мы можем реализовать **\\\"Emergency Recovery Code\\\"** (длинный буквенно-цифровой код), который шифрует мастер-ключ отдельно от биометрии. \\n\\u003e \\n\\u003e В финальной версии CLAVIS должен использовать **\\\"Identity Recovery Flow\\\"**: \\n\\u003e 1. Пользователь доказывает свою личность (например, через видео-интервью или личный визит в банк).\\n\\u003e 2. Эмитенты (Issuers) автоматически делают \\\"Push\\\" обновленных Verifiable Credentials на новый аппаратный ключ пользователя.\\n\\n**Как вы считаете, для австралийского рынка (с учетом падения продуктивности и любви к порядку) какой путь будет более приемлемым: «бумажный код в сейфе» или «автоматический перевыпуск через MyGov/Банк»?**\\n\\nWould you like me to add a detailed **Recovery Flow** section to the CLAVIS specification?\"],[null,null,null,null,[null,null,8]],null,null,null,true,null,[2],\"ru\",null,null,[null,null,null,null,null,null,[0],[]],null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[false],null,null,null,null,null,null,[],null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[10,10]]],null,null,\"rc_f85611d81076edd0\",null,null,null,null,\"AU\",null,null,null,[[[[null,[null,0,\"Это отличный вопрос, который вскрывает главную дилемму безопасности: «Чем сложнее украсть данные, тем легче их потерять самому».\",[[69,127,[[null,null,null,null,2]]]]]],[null,[null,0,\"Если мы используем setInvalidatedByBiometricEnrollment(true), мы превращаем смартфон в «черный ящик». аннулируется?\"],2,null,1,\"56fdd199312815e2\",0,2],[[[\"rc_a1dc9327f815b1f5\",[\"Да, вы абсолютно правы. Если этот флаг установлен в `true`, то **любое изменение** в списке зарегистрированных биометрических данных (добавление нового пальца, удаление старого или перенастройка распознавания лица) делает ключ в аппаратном хранилище (Keystore) **навсегда непригодным** для использования.\\n\\nВот как это работает и зачем это нужно для проекта **CLAVIS**:\\n\\n### Как это работает технически?\\n1. **Привязка к \\\"слепку\\\" системы:** Когда вы генерируете ключ с этим флагом, Keystore делает \\\"снимок\\\" текущего состояния биометрической базы данных устройства.\\n2. **Инвалидация:** Как только в настройках телефона добавляется хотя бы один новый отпечаток, Keystore видит, что база изменилась, и блокирует доступ к ключу. Попытка использовать его вызовет исключение `KeyPermanentlyInvalidatedException`.\\n3. **Результат:** Расшифровать данные старым ключом будет невозможно. Пользователю придется заново инициализировать CLAVIS (генерировать новый ключ) и, скорее всего, заново вводить свои данные (если не предусмотрен механизм восстановления).\\n\\n### Зачем это нужно (Сценарий кражи)?\\nЭтот флаг — критически важная защита от следующего сценария:\\n1. Злоумышленник узнает ваш **PIN-код** (подсмотрел в кафе).\\n2. Он крадет телефон. \\n3. Зная PIN, он заходит в настройки системы и **добавляет свой палец** в список разрешенных отпечатков.\\n4. Если бы этого флага не было, он смог бы открыть ваш CLAVIS своим пальцем и получить доступ ко всем вашим паспортам и медицинским картам.\\n5. С этим флагом — как только он добавит свой палец, ваш \\\"сейф\\\" (Keystore) самоблокируется.\\n\\n### Что это значит для пользователя?\\nЭто создает определенное неудобство: если вы решите добавить палец левой руки для удобства, вам придется перенастраивать приложение. Однако для системы уровня **CLAVIS**, которая претендует на хранение сверхчувствительных данных (паспорта, свидетельства о собственности), такая \\\"параноидальная\\\" защита считается золотым стандартом безопасности.\\n\\n**Стоит ли оставлять это в PoC?**\\nДля демонстрации концепции (PoC) это отличный пункт, чтобы показать инвесторам или техническим специалистам, насколько глубоко продумана безопасность: «Даже если кто-то узнает ваш пароль от телефона, ваши данные в CLAVIS все равно останутся в безопасности».\"],[null,null,null,null,[null,null,8]],null,null,null,true,null,[2],\"ru\",null,null,[null,null,null,null,null,null,[0],[]],null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[false],null,null,null,null,null,null,[],null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[[\"**Clarifying Biometric Implications**\\n\\nI'm currently focused on the `setInvalidatedByBiometricEnrollment(true)` flag within Android Keystore. I need to understand how biometric enrollment invalidates key usage, and how this flag controls that behavior. Генерирует ключ в `AndroidKeyStore`.\\n2. Использует `Cipher` в паре с `BiometricPrompt` для шифрования/дешифрования строки.\\n\\nЕсли хотите, я могу набросать структуру такого кода для Android?\"],[null,null,null,null,[null,null,8]],null,null,null,true,null,[2],\"ru\",null,null,[{\"7\":[0],\"8\":[],\"57\":[[[\"http://googleusercontent.com/immersive_entry_chip/0\"],\"c_32920e9e8337e92a_clavis_poc_roadmap.md\",null,\"CLAVIS: Proof of Concept Roadmap\",\"9a0a1f81-cea7-40af-9335-a1d0b5a24744\",[1774481932,509887221],true,1]]}],null,null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[false],null,null,null,null,null,null,[],null,[[\"c_32920e9e8337e92a_clavis_poc_roadmap.md\",\"e2ff597a-e56e-420a-ba35-ca16675c41a7\",\"CLAVIS: Proof of Concept Roadmap (v1.1)\",null,\"# Roadmap: Proof of Concept (PoC) для CLAVIS\\n\\nЦель данного PoC — продемонстрировать, что персональные данные физически защищены аппаратным модулем (TEE) и доступны только после биометрической авторизации, используя нативные средства Android.\\n\\n## 1. Стек технологий\\n* **Платформа:** Android (Kotlin).\\n* **Библиотека безопасности:** Jetpack Security (Crypto).\\n* **Хранилище:** EncryptedSharedPreferences (нативная обертка над Keystore).\\n* **Аутентификация:** BiometricPrompt API (Class 3 / Strong).\\n\\n## 2. Этапы реализации\\n\\n### Этап 1: Инициализация Master Key\\nПри первом запуске приложение создает `MasterKey` с использованием аппаратного Keystore:\\n* Ключ помечается как `setUserAuthenticationRequired(true)`.\\n* Добавляется флаг `setInvalidatedByBiometricEnrollment(true)` для защиты от несанкционированного добавления биометрии (Anti-Theft).\\n\\n### Этап 2: Настройка EncryptedSharedPreferences\\nВместо обычных файлов используется `EncryptedSharedPreferences`. Это позволяет:\\n* Автоматически шифровать ключи (имена полей) с помощью **AES256_SIV**.\\n* Автоматически шифровать значения (данные) с помощью **AES256_GCM**.\\n* Использовать аппаратный ключ из Этапа 1 для прозрачного доступа к данным.\\n\\n### Этап 3: Запись персональных данных (PII)\\n1. Пользователь вводит тестовые данные (например, \\\"Full Name\\\", \\\"Passport ID\\\").\\n2. Приложение сохраняет их через `sharedPrefs.edit().putString(...)`.\\n3. Система автоматически запрашивает биометрию для подтверждения записи (если настроено) и шифрует данные \\\"на лету\\\" перед сохранением в XML-файл настроек.\\n\\n### Этап 4: Симуляция \\\"Запроса от Verifier\\\"\\n1. На главном экране кнопка \\\"Предоставить данные\\\".\\n2. Вызывается системное окно `BiometricPrompt`.\\n3. **Результат:** Только после успешного сканирования отпечатка/лица приложение получает доступ к `EncryptedSharedPreferences`, считывает данные и отображает их на экране (имитируя готовность к передаче по протоколу CLAVIS).\\n\\n## 3. Преимущества этого подхода для PoC\\n1. **Нативность:** Мы используем стандартные инструменты Google, что повышает доверие к безопасности решения.\\n2. **Надежность:** Исключены ошибки ручного управления векторами инициализации (IV) и солями.\\n3. **Наглядность:** Легко показать в логах, что без биометрии попытка прочитать `SharedPrefs` вызывает ошибку доступа на уровне системы.\\n\\n## 4. Контрольные точки демонстрации\\n* Показать, что данные \\\"забываются\\\", если в настройках телефона изменить список отпечатков пальцев.\\n* Показать, что данные нельзя прочитать, просто скопировав файл настроек с телефона (они зашифрованы аппаратным ключом, привязанным к процессору).\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774490356,316034000],[1774481932,526665000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Roadmap: Proof of Concept (PoC) для CLAVIS\"]],[null,[null,0,\"Цель данного PoC — продемонстрировать, что персональные данные физически защищены аппаратным модулем (TEE) и доступны только после биометрической авторизации, используя нативные средства Android.\"]],[null,[null,2,\"1. Vault Inter-App Protocol (SVIAP)\",null,\"# Protocol Specification: Secure Vault Inter-App Protocol (SVIAP)\\n\\n## 1. Introduction\\nThe **Secure Vault Inter-App Protocol (SVIAP)** defines a platform-independent method for a **Holder** (the Vault application) to securely store, authorize, and transmit **Verifiable Credentials (VCs)** to **Verifiers** (external applications). The protocol mandates the use of a hardware-backed security module (HSM, TEE, SE, or TPM) for all cryptographic operations.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. Protocol Entities (DID Roles)\\n* **Holder (H):** The application managing the user's decentralized identity and hardware-protected keys.\\n* **Verifier (V):** An external application or service requesting a **Verifiable Presentation** of specific data points.\\n* **Issuer (I):** A trusted third party (e.g., a Doctor or Bank) that has cryptographically signed a credential.\\n* **Hardware Security Module (HSM):** The platform's secure environment used for key generation and biometric-gated operations.\\n* **Subject (User):** The individual to whom the credentials belong.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. Cryptographic Prerequisites\\n1.  **Decentralized Identifier (DID):** Each entity is identified by a unique URI (e.g., `did:sviap:user123`).\\n2.  **Holder Identity Keypair ($S_{H}, P_{H}$):** Asymmetric keys stored in the HSM used to sign Verifiable Presentations.\\n3.  **Verifier Encryption Key ($P_{V}$):** An ephemeral public key provided by the Verifier to secure the data transfer.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 4. Logical Dataflow (The VC Handshake)\\n\\n### Phase I: Credential Storage\\n1.  **Hardware Attestation:** $H$ verifies the device has a TEE/SE.\\n2.  **Encryption:** $I$ issues a signed VC. $H$ encrypts this VC using a hardware-protected master key ($K_{M}$) before saving to local storage.\\n\\n### Phase II: Presentation Request\\n1.  **Discovery:** $V$ sends a **Presentation Request** to $H$ containing:\\n    * `Input_Descriptors`: Specific fields required (e.g., `name`, `license_number`).\\n    * `Verifier_DID`: The identity of the requester.\\n    * `Public_Key_V`: $P_{V}$ for the secure delivery channel.\\n    * `Nonce`: A challenge to ensure freshness.\\n\\n### Phase III: Subject Authorization\\n1.  **Consent UI:** $H$ resolves the `Verifier_DID` against the **Trust Registry** (Entry #3 in Draft Notes) and prompts the user.\\n2.  **Biometric Gate:** The HSM releases the keys required to generate a Verifiable Presentation *only* upon successful biometric authentication.\\n\\n### Phase IV: Verifiable Presentation (VP) Delivery\\n1.  **VP Generation:** $H$ extracts the requested fields and signs them with $S_{H}$ to create a Verifiable Presentation.\\n2.  **Peer-to-Peer Encryption:** $H$ re-encrypts the VP using the Verifier's public key ($P_{V}$).\\n    * $Ciphertext \\u003d Encrypt_{P_{V}}(VP + Nonce)$\\n3.  **Transmission:** $H$ returns the encrypted VP to $V$.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 5. Security Principles\\n\\n### 5.1 Hardware-Bound Presentation\\nUnlike software-only wallets, the private key $S_{H}$ never leaves the TEE. A \\\"Presentation\\\" cannot be spoofed or replayed because the hardware requires a fresh biometric trigger for every unique `Nonce`.\\n\\n### 5.2 Purpose Binding \\u0026 Non-Repudiation\\nEvery VP includes a `Purpose_Statement` and an `Expiry_Timestamp`. Because the VP is signed by the Holder's hardware-backed key, it provides an immutable record of what was shared and why.\\n\\n### 5.3 Verifier Accountability\\nThe **Verifier** must provide a valid `did:sviap` that is cross-referenced with the **Operator Trust Hierarchy**. Unverified DIDs trigger \\\"Non-Registered\\\" warnings (Entry #3).\\n\\n---\\n*End of Protocol Specification*\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774323772,632690000],[1774311275,489327000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Protocol Specification: Secure Vault Inter-App Protocol (SVIAP)\"]],[null,[null,2,\"1. **Consumer** is widely used in software architecture (e.g., Producer-Consumer patterns), but in the context of Digital Identity and Cybersecurity, it might be slightly too generic or even misleading.\\n\\nIn the industry (specifically the W3C Verifiable Credentials and OIDC standards), there are more precise alternatives depending on the \\\"vibe\\\" you want the protocol to have:\\n\\n### 1. **\\\"Verifier\\\"** (The Industry Standard)\\nIn the world of **Verifiable Credentials (VC)** and **DIDs**, the entity that requests and checks data is strictly called the **Verifier**.\\n* **Pros:** It’s the standard technical term. It implies that the app isn't just \\\"consuming\\\" data, but is responsible for *verifying* its authenticity/signature.\\n* **Cons:** Can sound a bit academic.\\n\\n### 2. **\\\"Relying Party\\\" (RP)** (The Security Standard)\\nIn **OAuth2** and **OpenID Connect (OIDC)**, an application that relies on an Identity Provider (the Vault) to give it information is called the **Relying Party**.\\n* **Pros:** Very common in enterprise security. These notes act as a \\\"scratchpad\\\" for the user to capture brainstormed ideas which may later be referenced for formal implementation within the project's scope.\\n\\n## Protocol of Interaction\\n1.  **Trigger:** The user provides an idea, often prefaced by \\\"make a draft note\\\" or similar.\\n2.  **Processing:** If the input is in a language other than English, it will be translated. The content will then be polished for technical clarity and professional formatting.\\n3.  **Recording:** The refined thought will be appended to this file.\\n4.  **Reference:** These notes remain dormant until the user explicitly requests to integrate a specific idea into the project's formal specifications or protocols.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 📝 Draft Entries\\n\\n### Entry #1: The Australian Productivity \\u0026 Administrative Burden Case\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n(Content regarding form fatigue, labor productivity decline, and the $1.2B healthcare error cost.)\\n\\n### Entry #2: Taxonomy of Secure Data Types\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n(Content regarding Personal, Financial, Medical, Education, Property, and Residential data categories.)\\n\\n### Entry #3: The Operator Trust Hierarchy \\u0026 Verification Registry\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n(Content regarding Trusted, Registered, and Non-Registered operator categories.)\\n\\n### Entry #4: Data Retention, Expiry, \\u0026 Deletion Audit Protocol\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n(Content regarding the Lifecycle Contract, Audit Logs, and the \\\"Right to be Forgotten.\\\")\\n\\n### Entry #5: Web Interaction Protocol (Compliant vs. Legacy)\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n(Content regarding SVIAP-ready websites vs. predictive filling for legacy sites.)\\n\\n### Entry #6: Contextual Authorization \\u0026 Data-Operator Matching\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n(Content regarding domain-data affinity, anomaly flags, and multi-factor authentication for cross-domain requests.)\\n\\n### Entry #7: Lessons from the Pandemic – The Case for Structural Privacy\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n\\n**The Problem: \\\"Emergency Quality\\\" Solutions**\\nDuring the COVID-19 pandemic, Australia saw the rapid rollout of digital check-in systems (QR codes) that lacked a unified architectural vision. This led to:\\n* **\\\"Surveillance Creep\\\":** Law enforcement in several states (WA, QLD, VIC) accessed health check-in data for criminal investigations, breaking public trust.\\n* **Third-Party Fragmentation:** Businesses used unverified third-party platforms, creating unsecure \\\"honeypots\\\" of personal data.\\n* **Legal Instability:** Regulations were often changed by ministerial decree rather than primary legislation.\\n\\n**The Solution: SVIAP as a Universal Registration Protocol**\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** The Vault provides \\\"Ephemeral Handshakes\\\" rather than plaintext uploads.\\n* **Immutable Purpose Binding:** The protocol cryptographically binds data to a specific purpose (e.g., \\\"Health Check-in Only\\\"), preventing mission creep.\\n* **Verification Over Collection:** Allows a \\\"Green Tick\\\" status check without the venue needing to see or store the user's name/phone.\\n\\n### Entry #8: Integration with W3C DID \\u0026 Verifiable Credentials (VC)\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n\\nThe SVIAP protocol should be viewed as a high-security implementation of the **W3C Decentralized Identity (DID)** and **Verifiable Credentials (VC)** standards.\\n\\n**1. The \\\"Trust Triangle\\\" Mapping:**\\n* **Issuer:** The licensed professionals (Entry #3) who sign the data (e.g., a Doctor signing a medical record).\\n* **Holder:** The Secure Vault App, which stores the encrypted VC.\\n* **Verifier:** The \\\"Consumer\\\" app requesting the data.\\n\\n**2. Hardware-Enforced Credentials:**\\nWhile standard VCs are often software-managed, SVIAP mandates that the private keys used to \\\"Present\\\" a credential are locked in the **Hardware Keystore (TEE/SE)**. This creates a \\\"Hardware-Bound Presentation,\\\" ensuring the identity cannot be cloned even if the phone's OS is compromised.\\n\\n**3. Interoperability:**\\nBy aligning with the DID/VC standards, the Secure Vault can theoretically accept credentials from any international issuer (e.g., an EU Digital Identity Wallet) while adding a superior layer of hardware-backed authorization.\\n\\n---\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774323472,13019000],[1774323221,96068000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Draft Notes: Secure Vault Project\"]],[null,[null,2,\"Purpose Statement\"]],[null,[null,0,\"This document serves as a persistent repository for evolving thoughts, architectural ideas, and preliminary concepts related to the Secure Vault project. This led to:\\n* **\\\"Surveillance Creep\\\":** Law enforcement in several states (WA, QLD, VIC) accessed health check-in data for criminal investigations, breaking public trust.\\n* **Third-Party Fragmentation:** Businesses used unverified third-party platforms, creating unsecure \\\"honeypots\\\" of personal data.\\n* **Legal Instability:** Regulations were often changed by ministerial decree rather than primary legislation.\\n\\n**The Solution: SVIAP as a Universal Registration Protocol**\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** The Vault provides \\\"Ephemeral Handshakes\\\" rather than plaintext uploads.\\n* **Immutable Purpose Binding:** The protocol cryptographically binds data to a specific purpose (e.g., \\\"Health Check-in Only\\\"), preventing mission creep.\\n* **Verification Over Collection:** Allows a \\\"Green Tick\\\" status check without the venue needing to see or store the user's name/phone.\\n\\n### Entry #8: Integration with W3C DID \\u0026 Verifiable Credentials (VC)\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n\\nThe SVIAP protocol should be viewed as a high-security implementation of the **W3C Decentralized Identity (DID)** and **Verifiable Credentials (VC)** standards.\\n\\n**1. The \\\"Trust Triangle\\\" Mapping:**\\n* **Issuer:** The licensed professionals (Entry #3) who sign the data (e.g., a Doctor signing a medical record).\\n* **Holder:** The Secure Vault App, which stores the encrypted VC.\\n* **Verifier:** The \\\"Consumer\\\" app requesting the data.\\n\\n**2. Hardware-Enforced Credentials:**\\nWhile standard VCs are often software-managed, SVIAP mandates that the private keys used to \\\"Present\\\" a credential are locked in the **Hardware Keystore (TEE/SE)**. This creates a \\\"Hardware-Bound Presentation,\\\" ensuring the identity cannot be cloned even if the phone's OS is compromised.\\n\\n**3. This led to:\\n* **\\\"Surveillance Creep\\\":** Law enforcement in several states (WA, QLD, VIC) accessed health check-in data for criminal investigations, breaking public trust.\\n* **Third-Party Fragmentation:** Businesses used unverified third-party platforms, creating unsecure \\\"honeypots\\\" of personal data.\\n* **Legal Instability:** Regulations were often changed by ministerial decree rather than primary legislation.\\n\\n**The Solution: SVIAP as a Universal Registration Protocol**\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** The Vault provides \\\"Ephemeral Handshakes\\\" rather than plaintext uploads.\\n* **Immutable Purpose Binding:** The protocol cryptographically binds data to a specific purpose (e.g., \\\"Health Check-in Only\\\"), preventing mission creep.\\n* **Verification Over Collection:** Allows a \\\"Green Tick\\\" status check without the venue needing to see or store the user's name/phone.\\n\\n### Entry #8: Integration with W3C DID \\u0026 Verifiable Credentials (VC)\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n\\nThe SVIAP protocol should be viewed as a high-security implementation of the **W3C Decentralized Identity (DID)** and **Verifiable Credentials (VC)** standards.\\n\\n**1. The \\\"Trust Triangle\\\" Mapping:**\\n* **Issuer:** The licensed professionals (Entry #3) who sign the data (e.g., a Doctor signing a medical record).\\n* **Holder:** The Secure Vault App, which stores the encrypted VC.\\n* **Verifier:** The \\\"Consumer\\\" app requesting the data.\\n\\n**2. Hardware-Enforced Credentials:**\\nWhile standard VCs are often software-managed, SVIAP mandates that the private keys used to \\\"Present\\\" a credential are locked in the **Hardware Keystore (TEE/SE)**. This creates a \\\"Hardware-Bound Presentation,\\\" ensuring the identity cannot be cloned even if the phone's OS is compromised.\\n\\n**3. Interoperability:**\\nBy aligning with the DID/VC standards, the Secure Vault can theoretically accept credentials from any international issuer (e.g., an EU Digital Identity Wallet) while adding a superior layer of hardware-backed authorization.\\n\\n---\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774323472,13019000],[1774323221,96068000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Draft Notes: Secure Vault Project\"]],[null,[null,2,\"Purpose Statement\"]],[null,[null,0,\"This document serves as a persistent repository for evolving thoughts, architectural ideas, and preliminary concepts related to the Secure Vault project. This led to:\\n* **\\\"Surveillance Creep\\\":** Law enforcement in several states (WA, QLD, VIC) accessed health check-in data for criminal investigations, breaking public trust.\\n* **Third-Party Fragmentation:** Businesses used unverified third-party platforms, creating unsecure \\\"honeypots\\\" of personal data.\\n* **Legal Instability:** Regulations were often changed by ministerial decree rather than primary legislation.\\n\\n**The Solution: SVIAP as a Universal Registration Protocol**\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** The Vault provides \\\"Ephemeral Handshakes\\\" rather than plaintext uploads.\\n* **Immutable Purpose Binding:** The protocol cryptographically binds data to a specific purpose (e.g., \\\"Health Check-in Only\\\"), preventing mission creep.\\n* **Verification Over Collection:** Allows a \\\"Green Tick\\\" status check without the venue needing to see or store the user's name/phone.\\n\\n### Entry #8: Integration with W3C DID \\u0026 Verifiable Credentials (VC)\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n\\nThe SVIAP protocol should be viewed as a high-security implementation of the **W3C Decentralized Identity (DID)** and **Verifiable Credentials (VC)** standards.\\n\\n**1. The \\\"Trust Triangle\\\" Mapping:**\\n* **Issuer:** The licensed professionals (Entry #3) who sign the data (e.g., a Doctor signing a medical record).\\n* **Holder:** The Secure Vault App, which stores the encrypted VC.\\n* **Verifier:** The \\\"Consumer\\\" app requesting the data.\\n\\n**2. Hardware-Enforced Credentials:**\\nWhile standard VCs are often software-managed, SVIAP mandates that the private keys used to \\\"Present\\\" a credential are locked in the **Hardware Keystore (TEE/SE)**. This creates a \\\"Hardware-Bound Presentation,\\\" ensuring the identity cannot be cloned even if the phone's OS is compromised.\\n\\n**3. Interoperability:**\\nBy aligning with the DID/VC standards, the Secure Vault can theoretically accept credentials from any international issuer (e.g., an EU Digital Identity Wallet) while adding a superior layer of hardware-backed authorization.\\n\\n---\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774323472,13019000],[1774323221,96068000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Draft Notes: Secure Vault Project\"]],[null,[null,2,\"Purpose Statement\"]],[null,[null,0,\"This document serves as a persistent repository for evolving thoughts, architectural ideas, and preliminary concepts related to the Secure Vault project. This led to:\\n* **\\\"Surveillance Creep\\\":** Law enforcement in several states (WA, QLD, VIC) accessed health check-in data for criminal investigations, breaking public trust.\\n* **Third-Party Fragmentation:** Businesses used unverified third-party platforms, creating unsecure \\\"honeypots\\\" of personal data.\\n* **Legal Instability:** Regulations were often changed by ministerial decree rather than primary legislation.\\n\\n**The Solution: SVIAP as a Universal Registration Protocol**\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** The Vault provides \\\"Ephemeral Handshakes\\\" rather than plaintext uploads.\\n* **Immutable Purpose Binding:** The protocol cryptographically binds data to a specific purpose (e.g., \\\"Health Check-in Only\\\"), preventing mission creep.\\n* **Verification Over Collection:** Allows a \\\"Green Tick\\\" status check without the venue needing to see or store the user's name/phone.\\n\\n### Entry #8: Integration with W3C DID \\u0026 Verifiable Credentials (VC)\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n\\nThe SVIAP protocol should be viewed as a high-security implementation of the **W3C Decentralized Identity (DID)** and **Verifiable Credentials (VC)** standards.\\n\\n**1. The \\\"Trust Triangle\\\" Mapping:**\\n* **Issuer:** The licensed professionals (Entry #3) who sign the data (e.g., a Doctor signing a medical record).\\n* **Holder:** The Secure Vault App, which stores the encrypted VC.\\n* **Verifier:** The \\\"Consumer\\\" app requesting the data.\\n\\n**2. Hardware-Enforced Credentials:**\\nWhile standard VCs are often software-managed, SVIAP mandates that the private keys used to \\\"Present\\\" a credential are locked in the **Hardware Keystore (TEE/SE)**. This creates a \\\"Hardware-Bound Presentation,\\\" ensuring the identity cannot be cloned even if the phone's OS is compromised.\\n\\n**3. Interoperability:**\\nBy aligning with the DID/VC standards, the Secure Vault can theoretically accept credentials from any international issuer (e.g., an EU Digital Identity Wallet) while adding a superior layer of hardware-backed authorization.\\n\\n---\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774323472,13019000],[1774323221,96068000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Draft Notes: Secure Vault Project\"]],[null,[null,2,\"Purpose Statement\"]],[null,[null,0,\"This document serves as a persistent repository for evolving thoughts, architectural ideas, and preliminary concepts related to the Secure Vault project. This led to:\\n* **\\\"Surveillance Creep\\\":** Law enforcement in several states (WA, QLD, VIC) accessed health check-in data for criminal investigations, breaking public trust.\\n* **Third-Party Fragmentation:** Businesses used unverified third-party platforms, creating unsecure \\\"honeypots\\\" of personal data.\\n* **Legal Instability:** Regulations were often changed by ministerial decree rather than primary legislation.\\n\\n**The Solution: SVIAP as a Universal Registration Protocol**\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** The Vault provides \\\"Ephemeral Handshakes\\\" rather than plaintext uploads.\\n* **Immutable Purpose Binding:** The protocol cryptographically binds data to a specific purpose (e.g., \\\"Health Check-in Only\\\"), preventing mission creep.\\n* **Verification Over Collection:** Allows a \\\"Green Tick\\\" status check without the venue needing to see or store the user's name/phone.\\n\\n### Entry #8: Integration with W3C DID \\u0026 Verifiable Credentials (VC)\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n\\nThe SVIAP protocol should be viewed as a high-security implementation of the **W3C Decentralized Identity (DID)** and **Verifiable Credentials (VC)** standards.\\n\\n**1. The \\\"Trust Triangle\\\" Mapping:**\\n* **Issuer:** The licensed professionals (Entry #3) who sign the data (e.g., a Doctor signing a medical record).\\n* **Holder:** The Secure Vault App, which stores the encrypted VC.\\n* **Verifier:** The \\\"Consumer\\\" app requesting the data.\\n\\n**2. Hardware-Enforced Credentials:**\\nWhile standard VCs are often software-managed, SVIAP mandates that the private keys used to \\\"Present\\\" a credential are locked in the **Hardware Keystore (TEE/SE)**. This creates a \\\"Hardware-Bound Presentation,\\\" ensuring the identity cannot be cloned even if the phone's OS is compromised.\\n\\n**3. This led to:\\n* **\\\"Surveillance Creep\\\":** Law enforcement in several states (WA, QLD, VIC) accessed health check-in data for criminal investigations, breaking public trust.\\n* **Third-Party Fragmentation:** Businesses used unverified third-party platforms, creating unsecure \\\"honeypots\\\" of personal data.\\n* **Legal Instability:** Regulations were often changed by ministerial decree rather than primary legislation.\\n\\n**The Solution: SVIAP as a Universal Registration Protocol**\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** The Vault provides \\\"Ephemeral Handshakes\\\" rather than plaintext uploads.\\n* **Immutable Purpose Binding:** The protocol cryptographically binds data to a specific purpose (e.g., \\\"Health Check-in Only\\\"), preventing mission creep.\\n* **Verification Over Collection:** Allows a \\\"Green Tick\\\" status check without the venue needing to see or store the user's name/phone.\\n\\n### Entry #8: Integration with W3C DID \\u0026 Verifiable Credentials (VC)\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n\\nThe SVIAP protocol should be viewed as a high-security implementation of the **W3C Decentralized Identity (DID)** and **Verifiable Credentials (VC)** standards.\\n\\n**1. The \\\"Trust Triangle\\\" Mapping:**\\n* **Issuer:** The licensed professionals (Entry #3) who sign the data (e.g., a Doctor signing a medical record).\\n* **Holder:** The Secure Vault App, which stores the encrypted VC.\\n* **Verifier:** The \\\"Consumer\\\" app requesting the data.\\n\\n**2. Hardware-Enforced Credentials:**\\nWhile standard VCs are often software-managed, SVIAP mandates that the private keys used to \\\"Present\\\" a credential are locked in the **Hardware Keystore (TEE/SE)**. I was using \\\"TEE\\\" as a shorthand for the entire secure storage chain, but in a technical review, that distinction is critical.\\n\\nThe **TEE (Trusted Execution Environment)** or **Secure Element (SE)** is primarily designed for high-security processing and key management. It is not built to store large amounts of unstructured personal data (like a full medical history or a scanned PDF of a passport).\\n\\nI have updated the **Market Review** to accurately reflect how these systems actually handle data vs. keys.\\n\\n\\nhttp://googleusercontent.com/immersive_entry_chip/0\\n\\n### Key Technical Clarification:\\n* **The TEE/SE** is the \\\"Lock and Key.\\\" It stores the private keys and performs the \\\"signing\\\" or \\\"decryption\\\" operations.\\n* **The App Storage** is the \\\"Vault.\\\" It holds the encrypted data.\\n* **The Handshake:** When you approve with a fingerprint, the TEE releases the key to decrypt the data in the app's memory just long enough to re-encrypt it for the recipient.\\n\\nI've updated the file to be technically accurate. Current Landscape Overview\\nAs of current market analysis, there is no single \\\"universal\\\" application that functions as a general-purpose, hardware-locked broker for third-party data requests. The market is fragmented into three primary categories, each implementing specific components of the proposed Secure Vault logic.\\n\\n## 2. Competitive Analysis\\n\\n### 2.1 Digital Identity Wallets (The \\\"Protocol\\\" Precursor)\\n* **Examples:** European Digital Identity Wallet (EUDI), Samsung Wallet, Apple Wallet IDs.\\n* **Storage Logic (Correction):** These apps do **not** store the bulk identity data *inside* the TEE.\\n    * **TEE/Secure Element:** Stores the **private cryptographic keys** and handles the biometric matching logic.\\n    * **Encrypted Storage:** The actual identity data (name, DOB, passport number) is stored in the app's private, encrypted database on the standard flash storage, but the **decryption key** is locked inside the TEE.\\n* **Gaps:** Restricted to official government-issued documents. They do not support user-defined \\\"custom\\\" records (e.g., private lease agreements or specific medical notes) for general third-party application use.\\n\\n### 2.2 Hardware-Backed Password Managers (The \\\"Storage\\\" Precursor)\\n* **Examples:** Bitwarden, 1Password, SecureX.\\n* **Strengths:** Excellent use of HSMs to protect the \\\"Master Key.\\\"\\n* **Gaps:** Primarily use \\\"Push\\\" technology (Autofill). They lack a secure bidirectional handshake protocol where data is re-encrypted specifically for a recipient's public key before transmission.\\n\\n### 2.3 Platform Credential Managers (The \\\"Auth\\\" Precursor)\\n* **Examples:** Android Credential Manager, Apple Passkeys.\\n* **Strengths:** Uses hardware-backed keys to sign challenges for app logins.\\n* **Gaps:** Focus strictly on *authentication* (identity verification) rather than *data exchange* (sharing specific encrypted records).\\n\\n## 3. The \\\"Missing Link\\\": Why it doesn't exist yet\\nSeveral barriers have prevented the emergence of a universal vault:\\n1.  **Trust Gap:** Consumer apps need \\\"Remote Attestation\\\" to prove the Vault is genuine.\\n2.  **App Sandboxing:** OS designs restrict Inter-Process Communication (IPC), making standardized data exchange difficult without a custom protocol.\\n3.  **Walled Gardens:** Platform holders (Google/Apple) prefer to act as the primary identity providers, limiting third-party \\\"Identity Brokers\\\" from accessing native biometric hooks for external apps.\\n\\n## 4. Market Opportunity: The Universal Data Broker\\nThe proposed specification describes a **Self-Sovereign Identity (SSI) Agent**. The unique opportunity lies in:\\n* **SVIAP Protocol:** Standardizing how any app requests data securely.\\n* **Data Sovereignty:** Moving beyond login credentials to allow users to own and share structured, encrypted personal data.\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** Ensuring that even the host OS cannot intercept the plaintext data during the exchange between the Vault and the Consumer.\\n\\n---\\n*End of Market Review*\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774312949,862980000],[1774312148,58424000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Market Review: Secure Data Vault Solutions\"]],[null,[null,2,\"1. This led to:\\n* **\\\"Surveillance Creep\\\":** Law enforcement in several states (WA, QLD, VIC) accessed health check-in data for criminal investigations, breaking public trust.\\n* **Third-Party Fragmentation:** Businesses used unverified third-party platforms, creating unsecure \\\"honeypots\\\" of personal data.\\n* **Legal Instability:** Regulations were often changed by ministerial decree rather than primary legislation.\\n\\n**The Solution: SVIAP as a Universal Registration Protocol**\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** The Vault provides \\\"Ephemeral Handshakes\\\" rather than plaintext uploads.\\n* **Immutable Purpose Binding:** The protocol cryptographically binds data to a specific purpose (e.g., \\\"Health Check-in Only\\\"), preventing mission creep.\\n* **Verification Over Collection:** Allows a \\\"Green Tick\\\" status check without the venue needing to see or store the user's name/phone.\\n\\n### Entry #8: Integration with W3C DID \\u0026 Verifiable Credentials (VC)\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n\\nThe SVIAP protocol should be viewed as a high-security implementation of the **W3C Decentralized Identity (DID)** and **Verifiable Credentials (VC)** standards.\\n\\n**1. The \\\"Trust Triangle\\\" Mapping:**\\n* **Issuer:** The licensed professionals (Entry #3) who sign the data (e.g., a Doctor signing a medical record).\\n* **Holder:** The Secure Vault App, which stores the encrypted VC.\\n* **Verifier:** The \\\"Consumer\\\" app requesting the data.\\n\\n**2. Hardware-Enforced Credentials:**\\nWhile standard VCs are often software-managed, SVIAP mandates that the private keys used to \\\"Present\\\" a credential are locked in the **Hardware Keystore (TEE/SE)**. This creates a \\\"Hardware-Bound Presentation,\\\" ensuring the identity cannot be cloned even if the phone's OS is compromised.\\n\\n**3. Interoperability:**\\nBy aligning with the DID/VC standards, the Secure Vault can theoretically accept credentials from any international issuer (e.g., an EU Digital Identity Wallet) while adding a superior layer of hardware-backed authorization.\\n\\n---\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774323472,13019000],[1774323221,96068000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Draft Notes: Secure Vault Project\"]],[null,[null,2,\"Purpose Statement\"]],[null,[null,0,\"This document serves as a persistent repository for evolving thoughts, architectural ideas, and preliminary concepts related to the Secure Vault project. This led to:\\n* **\\\"Surveillance Creep\\\":** Law enforcement in several states (WA, QLD, VIC) accessed health check-in data for criminal investigations, breaking public trust.\\n* **Third-Party Fragmentation:** Businesses used unverified third-party platforms, creating unsecure \\\"honeypots\\\" of personal data.\\n* **Legal Instability:** Regulations were often changed by ministerial decree rather than primary legislation.\\n\\n**The Solution: SVIAP as a Universal Registration Protocol**\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** The Vault provides \\\"Ephemeral Handshakes\\\" rather than plaintext uploads.\\n* **Immutable Purpose Binding:** The protocol cryptographically binds data to a specific purpose (e.g., \\\"Health Check-in Only\\\"), preventing mission creep.\\n* **Verification Over Collection:** Allows a \\\"Green Tick\\\" status check without the venue needing to see or store the user's name/phone.\\n\\n### Entry #8: Integration with W3C DID \\u0026 Verifiable Credentials (VC)\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n\\nThe SVIAP protocol should be viewed as a high-security implementation of the **W3C Decentralized Identity (DID)** and **Verifiable Credentials (VC)** standards.\\n\\n**1. The \\\"Trust Triangle\\\" Mapping:**\\n* **Issuer:** The licensed professionals (Entry #3) who sign the data (e.g., a Doctor signing a medical record).\\n* **Holder:** The Secure Vault App, which stores the encrypted VC.\\n* **Verifier:** The \\\"Consumer\\\" app requesting the data.\\n\\n**2. Hardware-Enforced Credentials:**\\nWhile standard VCs are often software-managed, SVIAP mandates that the private keys used to \\\"Present\\\" a credential are locked in the **Hardware Keystore (TEE/SE)**. This creates a \\\"Hardware-Bound Presentation,\\\" ensuring the identity cannot be cloned even if the phone's OS is compromised.\\n\\n**3. Interoperability:**\\nBy aligning with the DID/VC standards, the Secure Vault can theoretically accept credentials from any international issuer (e.g., an EU Digital Identity Wallet) while adding a superior layer of hardware-backed authorization.\\n\\n---\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774323472,13019000],[1774323221,96068000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Draft Notes: Secure Vault Project\"]],[null,[null,2,\"Purpose Statement\"]],[null,[null,0,\"This document serves as a persistent repository for evolving thoughts, architectural ideas, and preliminary concepts related to the Secure Vault project. Current Landscape Overview\\nAs of current market analysis, there is no single \\\"universal\\\" application that functions as a general-purpose, hardware-locked broker for third-party data requests. The market is fragmented into three primary categories, each implementing specific components of the proposed Secure Vault logic.\\n\\n## 2. Competitive Analysis\\n\\n### 2.1 Digital Identity Wallets (The \\\"Protocol\\\" Precursor)\\n* **Examples:** European Digital Identity Wallet (EUDI), Samsung Wallet, Apple Wallet IDs.\\n* **Storage Logic (Correction):** These apps do **not** store the bulk identity data *inside* the TEE.\\n    * **TEE/Secure Element:** Stores the **private cryptographic keys** and handles the biometric matching logic.\\n    * **Encrypted Storage:** The actual identity data (name, DOB, passport number) is stored in the app's private, encrypted database on the standard flash storage, but the **decryption key** is locked inside the TEE.\\n* **Gaps:** Restricted to official government-issued documents. They do not support user-defined \\\"custom\\\" records (e.g., private lease agreements or specific medical notes) for general third-party application use.\\n\\n### 2.2 Hardware-Backed Password Managers (The \\\"Storage\\\" Precursor)\\n* **Examples:** Bitwarden, 1Password, SecureX.\\n* **Strengths:** Excellent use of HSMs to protect the \\\"Master Key.\\\"\\n* **Gaps:** Primarily use \\\"Push\\\" technology (Autofill). They lack a secure bidirectional handshake protocol where data is re-encrypted specifically for a recipient's public key before transmission.\\n\\n### 2.3 Platform Credential Managers (The \\\"Auth\\\" Precursor)\\n* **Examples:** Android Credential Manager, Apple Passkeys.\\n* **Strengths:** Uses hardware-backed keys to sign challenges for app logins.\\n* **Gaps:** Focus strictly on *authentication* (identity verification) rather than *data exchange* (sharing specific encrypted records).\\n\\n## 3. The \\\"Missing Link\\\": Why it doesn't exist yet\\nSeveral barriers have prevented the emergence of a universal vault:\\n1.  **Trust Gap:** Consumer apps need \\\"Remote Attestation\\\" to prove the Vault is genuine.\\n2.  **App Sandboxing:** OS designs restrict Inter-Process Communication (IPC), making standardized data exchange difficult without a custom protocol.\\n3.  **Walled Gardens:** Platform holders (Google/Apple) prefer to act as the primary identity providers, limiting third-party \\\"Identity Brokers\\\" from accessing native biometric hooks for external apps.\\n\\n## 4. Market Opportunity: The Universal Data Broker\\nThe proposed specification describes a **Self-Sovereign Identity (SSI) Agent**. The unique opportunity lies in:\\n* **SVIAP Protocol:** Standardizing how any app requests data securely.\\n* **Data Sovereignty:** Moving beyond login credentials to allow users to own and share structured, encrypted personal data.\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** Ensuring that even the host OS cannot intercept the plaintext data during the exchange between the Vault and the Consumer.\\n\\n---\\n*End of Market Review*\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774312949,862980000],[1774312148,58424000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Market Review: Secure Data Vault Solutions\"]],[null,[null,2,\"1. These notes act as a \\\"scratchpad\\\" for the user to capture brainstormed ideas which may later be referenced for formal implementation within the project's scope.\\n\\n## Protocol of Interaction\\n1.  **Trigger:** The user provides an idea, often prefaced by \\\"make a draft note\\\" or similar.\\n2.  **Processing:** If the input is in a language other than English, it will be translated. This led to:\\n* **\\\"Surveillance Creep\\\":** Law enforcement in several states (WA, QLD, VIC) accessed health check-in data for criminal investigations, breaking public trust.\\n* **Third-Party Fragmentation:** Businesses used unverified third-party platforms, creating unsecure \\\"honeypots\\\" of personal data.\\n* **Legal Instability:** Regulations were often changed by ministerial decree rather than primary legislation.\\n\\n**The Solution: SVIAP as a Universal Registration Protocol**\\n* **Privacy-by-Design:** The Vault provides \\\"Ephemeral Handshakes\\\" rather than plaintext uploads.\\n* **Immutable Purpose Binding:** The protocol cryptographically binds data to a specific purpose (e.g., \\\"Health Check-in Only\\\"), preventing mission creep.\\n* **Verification Over Collection:** Allows a \\\"Green Tick\\\" status check without the venue needing to see or store the user's name/phone.\\n\\n### Entry #8: Integration with W3C DID \\u0026 Verifiable Credentials (VC)\\n*Date: March 24, 2026*\\n\\nThe SVIAP protocol should be viewed as a high-security implementation of the **W3C Decentralized Identity (DID)** and **Verifiable Credentials (VC)** standards.\\n\\n**1. The \\\"Trust Triangle\\\" Mapping:**\\n* **Issuer:** The licensed professionals (Entry #3) who sign the data (e.g., a Doctor signing a medical record).\\n* **Holder:** The Secure Vault App, which stores the encrypted VC.\\n* **Verifier:** The \\\"Consumer\\\" app requesting the data.\\n\\n**2. Hardware-Enforced Credentials:**\\nWhile standard VCs are often software-managed, SVIAP mandates that the private keys used to \\\"Present\\\" a credential are locked in the **Hardware Keystore (TEE/SE)**. This creates a \\\"Hardware-Bound Presentation,\\\" ensuring the identity cannot be cloned even if the phone's OS is compromised.\\n\\n**3. Interoperability:**\\nBy aligning with the DID/VC standards, the Secure Vault can theoretically accept credentials from any international issuer (e.g., an EU Digital Identity Wallet) while adding a superior layer of hardware-backed authorization.\\n\\n---\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774323472,13019000],[1774323221,96068000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Draft Notes: Secure Vault Project\"]],[null,[null,2,\"Purpose Statement\"]],[null,[null,0,\"This document serves as a persistent repository for evolving thoughts, architectural ideas, and preliminary concepts related to the Secure Vault project. Your concept essentially merges these three into one \\\"Super Vault.\\\"\\n\\n### 1. Digital Identity Wallets (The closest logic)\\nApps like the **European Digital Identity Wallet (EUDI)**, **Samsung Wallet**, or state-specific ID apps (like **Apple Wallet IDs** in the US) follow your logic most closely.\\n* **What they do:** They store government-issued IDs (passports, driver's licenses) in the TEE/Secure Element.\\n* **The Request:** When you check into a hotel or go through TSA, they use a protocol (ISO 18013-5) where the \\\"Consumer\\\" (the hotel app) requests data, and you authorize it via FaceID/Fingerprint.\\n* **The Gap:** These are currently limited to official documents. They don't allow you to store \\\"custom\\\" medical records or private notes for use by *any* random third-party app.\\n\\n### 2. Hardware-Backed Password Managers\\nApps like **SecureX**, **Bitwarden**, or **1Password** (when configured for \\\"Biometric Unlock\\\").\\n* **What they do:** They use the Android Keystore to protect the master key. This document focuses on the logical dataflow and security handshakes required for any system featuring a Hardware Security Module (HSM), TEE, or Secure Element.\\n\\n\\nhttp://googleusercontent.com/immersive_entry_chip/0\\n\\n### Highlights of this protocol:\\n* **Platform Agnostic:** It replaces Android-specific terms with general concepts like \\\"Consumer,\\\" \\\"Vault,\\\" and \\\"HSM.\\\"\\n* **Hardware Binding:** It emphasizes that the decryption of the Master Key must be physically/logically gated by the hardware's biometric check.\\n* **Ephemeral Encryption:** It uses the Consumer's public key to ensure that the data is encrypted \\\"for a specific pair of eyes\\\" before it ever leaves the Vault.\\n\\nWould you like me to expand on the **Attestation** part (how the Vault proves to the Consumer that it is actually a secure vault and not a fake app)?\"],[null,null,null,null,[null,null,7]],null,null,null,true,null,[2],\"en\",null,null,[{\"7\":[0],\"8\":[],\"57\":[[[\"http://googleusercontent.com/immersive_entry_chip/0\"],\"c_32920e9e8337e92a_sviap_protocol_v1.md\",null,\"Secure Vault Inter-App Protocol (SVIAP)\",\"c9ee60c8-a155-4c86-bf30-0772b6176a7e\",[1774311275,467545105],true,1]]}],null,null,true,null,null,null,null,null,[false],null,null,null,true,null,null,[],null,[[\"c_32920e9e8337e92a_sviap_protocol_v1.md\",\"f6bd1c7e-285c-4d37-9f57-5fac0c06b2e9\",\"Secure Vault Inter-App Protocol (SVIAP)\",null,\"# Protocol Specification: Secure Vault Inter-App Protocol (SVIAP)\\n\\n## 1. Introduction\\nThe **Secure Vault Inter-App Protocol (SVIAP)** defines a platform-independent method for a **Holder** (the Vault application) to securely store, authorize, and transmit **Verifiable Credentials (VCs)** to **Verifiers** (external applications). The protocol mandates the use of a hardware-backed security module (HSM, TEE, SE, or TPM) for all cryptographic operations.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. Protocol Entities (DID Roles)\\n* **Holder (H):** The application managing the user's decentralized identity and hardware-protected keys.\\n* **Verifier (V):** An external application or service requesting a **Verifiable Presentation** of specific data points.\\n* **Issuer (I):** A trusted third party (e.g., a Doctor or Bank) that has cryptographically signed a credential.\\n* **Hardware Security Module (HSM):** The platform's secure environment used for key generation and biometric-gated operations.\\n* **Subject (User):** The individual to whom the credentials belong.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. Cryptographic Prerequisites\\n1.  **Decentralized Identifier (DID):** Each entity is identified by a unique URI (e.g., `did:sviap:user123`).\\n2.  **Holder Identity Keypair ($S_{H}, P_{H}$):** Asymmetric keys stored in the HSM used to sign Verifiable Presentations.\\n3.  **Verifier Encryption Key ($P_{V}$):** An ephemeral public key provided by the Verifier to secure the data transfer.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 4. Logical Dataflow (The VC Handshake)\\n\\n### Phase I: Credential Storage\\n1.  **Hardware Attestation:** $H$ verifies the device has a TEE/SE.\\n2.  **Encryption:** $I$ issues a signed VC. $H$ encrypts this VC using a hardware-protected master key ($K_{M}$) before saving to local storage.\\n\\n### Phase II: Presentation Request\\n1.  **Discovery:** $V$ sends a **Presentation Request** to $H$ containing:\\n    * `Input_Descriptors`: Specific fields required (e.g., `name`, `license_number`).\\n    * `Verifier_DID`: The identity of the requester.\\n    * `Public_Key_V`: $P_{V}$ for the secure delivery channel.\\n    * `Nonce`: A challenge to ensure freshness.\\n\\n### Phase III: Subject Authorization\\n1.  **Consent UI:** $H$ resolves the `Verifier_DID` against the **Trust Registry** (Entry #3 in Draft Notes) and prompts the user.\\n2.  **Biometric Gate:** The HSM releases the keys required to generate a Verifiable Presentation *only* upon successful biometric authentication.\\n\\n### Phase IV: Verifiable Presentation (VP) Delivery\\n1.  **VP Generation:** $H$ extracts the requested fields and signs them with $S_{H}$ to create a Verifiable Presentation.\\n2.  **Peer-to-Peer Encryption:** $H$ re-encrypts the VP using the Verifier's public key ($P_{V}$).\\n    * $Ciphertext \\u003d Encrypt_{P_{V}}(VP + Nonce)$\\n3.  **Transmission:** $H$ returns the encrypted VP to $V$.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 5. Security Principles\\n\\n### 5.1 Hardware-Bound Presentation\\nUnlike software-only wallets, the private key $S_{H}$ never leaves the TEE. A \\\"Presentation\\\" cannot be spoofed or replayed because the hardware requires a fresh biometric trigger for every unique `Nonce`.\\n\\n### 5.2 Purpose Binding \\u0026 Non-Repudiation\\nEvery VP includes a `Purpose_Statement` and an `Expiry_Timestamp`. Because the VP is signed by the Holder's hardware-backed key, it provides an immutable record of what was shared and why.\\n\\n### 5.3 Verifier Accountability\\nThe **Verifier** must provide a valid `did:sviap` that is cross-referenced with the **Operator Trust Hierarchy**. Unverified DIDs trigger \\\"Non-Registered\\\" warnings (Entry #3).\\n\\n---\\n*End of Protocol Specification*\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774323772,632690000],[1774311275,489327000],1,true,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Protocol Specification: Secure Vault Inter-App Protocol (SVIAP)\"]],[null,[null,2,\"1. Introduction\"]],[null,[null,0,\"The Secure Vault Inter-App Protocol (SVIAP) defines a platform-independent method for a Holder (the Vault application) to securely store, authorize, and transmit Verifiable Credentials (VCs) to Verifiers (external applications). Приложение будет хранить личные данные пользователей в зашифрованном виде и предоставлять их другим приложениям только после явного подтверждения и авторизации пользователя.\\nОсновные требования\\nАппаратное хранилище (Keystore):\\nПриложение должно работать только на устройствах с аппаратной поддержкой Keystore (то есть, с наличием TEE или аналогичного модуля). Если аппаратная поддержка отсутствует, приложение не запускается и сообщает пользователю, что его устройство не поддерживается.\\nХранение личных данных:\\nЛичные данные (номера паспортов, медицинские записи и прочее) не хранятся в открытом виде. Вместо этого они шифруются ключами, которые генерируются и хранятся внутри Keystore. Приложение отвечает за шифрование и дешифрование данных, причём приватные ключи никогда не покидают защищённое окружение.\\nПроцесс подтверждения запроса:\\nКогда другое приложение запрашивает доступ к данным, пользователь должен явно подтвердить этот запрос. Подтверждение происходит через биометрическую аутентификацию (отпечаток пальца, распознавание лица) и/или через ввод PIN-кода. Можно предусмотреть многофакторную аутентификацию, например комбинацию биометрии и PIN-кода.\\nВыдача данных в зашифрованном виде:\\nДанные никогда не передаются в открытом виде. После подтверждения запроса данные шифруются для передачи с использованием ключа, принадлежащего запрашивающему приложению. Таким образом, только запрашивающая сторона, имея свой приватный ключ, сможет расшифровать полученные данные.\\nЗащита от массовой выгрузки:\\nДля предотвращения ситуации, когда кто-то попытается выгрузить все данные разом, можно реализовать ограничение на количество данных, выдаваемых за одну авторизацию, или вводить временные интервалы. Пользователь должен будет подтверждать каждую выдачу отдельно и явно, чтобы избежать автоматического массового экспорта.\\nИтоговая схема\\nГенерация ключей: В начале работы создаются ключи в Keystore.\\nШифрование данных: Личные данные шифруются и хранятся в зашифрованном виде.\\nЗапрос и подтверждение: Другое приложение запрашивает данные, пользователь подтверждает запрос через биометрию или PIN-код.\\nПередача данных: Данные шифруются ключом получателя и передаются в зашифрованном виде.\\nРасшифровка получателем: Только тот, у кого есть соответствующий приватный ключ, сможет расшифровать полученные данные.\\nВот такая спецификация. Overview\\nThis specification outlines the architecture and requirements for an Android application designed to serve as a high-security personal data vault. The application leverages hardware-backed security (Android Keystore) to store sensitive user information and provides a secure mechanism for sharing this data with authorized third-party applications only upon explicit user consent.\\n\\n## 2. Core Requirements\\n\\n### 2.1 Hardware-Backed Security (Android Keystore)\\n* **Mandatory Hardware Attestation:** The application must strictly require a Trusted Execution Environment (TEE) or a Secure Element (StrongBox).\\n* **Device Compatibility Check:** Upon launch, the app must verify the presence of hardware-backed key storage. If the device only supports software-based encryption, the application must terminate and notify the user that the device is unsupported for security reasons.\\n\\n### 2.2 Encrypted Personal Data Storage\\n* **Data Types:** Sensitive information includes, but is not limited to, passport details, medical records, and financial identifiers.\\n* **Encryption Standard:** All data must be stored in an encrypted format using industry-standard protocols (e.g., AES-GCM).\\n* **Key Isolation:** Encryption keys must be generated and stored within the Android Keystore. Private keys must be flagged as \\\"non-exportable,\\\" ensuring they never leave the hardware-protected environment.\\n* **Decryption Logic:** Decryption of stored data occurs only within the application's memory during an authorized session.\\n\\n### 2.3 User Authorization \\u0026 Authentication\\n* **Explicit Consent:** Access requests from external applications must trigger a system-level authorization prompt.\\n* **Authentication Methods:** The user must authorize each request using:\\n    * Biometric Authentication (Fingerprint, Face Unlock via Class 3/Strong Biometrics).\\n    * Device PIN/Password.\\n* **Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA):** The system should support optional MFA configurations, requiring both biometrics and a PIN for high-sensitivity data access.\\n\\n### 2.4 Secure Inter-App Data Transfer\\n* **No Plaintext Exposure:** Data must never be passed between applications in an unencrypted state.\\n* **Recipient-Specific Encryption:** Once a request is authorized, the Vault application encrypts the requested data using the public key provided by the requesting (third-party) application.\\n* **End-to-End Security:** Only the specific requesting application, possessing the corresponding private key, will be able to decrypt and utilize the transferred data.\\n\\n### 2.5 Protection Against Mass Data Extraction\\n* **Rate Limiting:** Implement strict controls on the volume of data that can be exported in a single session or timeframe.\\n* **Individual Authorization:** Every discrete data point or record request requires its own explicit user confirmation to prevent automated \\\"bulk\\\" harvesting of personal information.\\n* **Time Intervals:** Optional \\\"cooldown\\\" periods between sensitive data exports to mitigate the risk of forced or accidental mass exposure.\\n\\n## 3. Operational Workflow\\n\\n1.  **Key Initialization:** Upon the first run, the application generates master encryption keys within the hardware Keystore.\\n2.  **Data Ingestion:** User inputs personal data; the app encrypts it using the master keys and saves it to local persistent storage.\\n3.  **Inbound Request:** A third-party app requests specific data fields.\\n4.  **Authorization:** The Vault app interrupts the process, prompting the user for biometric or PIN confirmation.\\n5.  **Secure Packaging:** After successful authentication, the Vault app fetches the data, decrypts it internally, then re-encrypts it using the recipient's public key.\\n6.  **Transmission:** The encrypted payload is sent to the requesting app.\\n7.  **Final Decryption:** The recipient application uses its internal private key to access the data.\\n\\n---\\n*End of Specification*\",[null,null,null,null,null,null,null,[]],null,[[1774310855,689315000],null,1,false,false],[[[null,[null,1,\"Technical Specification: Secure Android Data Vault\"]],[null,[null,2,\"1.
